realizing his difficulty managing health routines was beginning to threaten both his independence and his ability to keep leading his community activities.\n- Chief Complaint: “I keep forgetting my I constantly feel restless, like there's a motor running inside me, which makes me fidget, pace, and struggle to sit still during appointments. My mind is always drifting off to other things, and I constantly catch myself interrupting people or jumping ahead before they can even finish their sentences.\n\nDoctor: Do you also often lose things, make careless mistakes, or have trouble finishing tasks because you get distracted, even when the task is important?\n\nPatient: Yes, I really struggle to finish tasks because I get so easily distracted. For instance, I'll sit down to write my daily blood pressure numbers, but then a bird outside the window or a joke from game night catches my attention, and I wander off before ever writing them down.\n\nDoctor: As a child, were you mainly inattentive and forgetful, or were you also very hyperactive and impulsive, such as constantly running/climbing, blurting out answers, or having trouble waiting your turn?\n\nPatient: Oh, I was definitely very hyperactive and impulsive back then. While he had always been a laid‑back, on‑the‑go sort of person, the increased medical demands after a mild cardiac scare about 14 months ago exposed how much he struggled to keep track of scheduled visits, instructions, and home monitoring, especially as he juggled organizing repair projects and game nights at the senior center. realizing his difficulty managing health routines was beginning to threaten both his independence and his ability to keep leading his community activities.\n- Chief Complaint: “I keep forgetting my word in or steer me back to the actual reason I’m there.\n- S018 (Interrupting_Intruding): When my nieces are trying to go over my pill boxes or check my online chart with me, I keep jumping in over them, finishing their sentences, grabbing the phone out of their hands to ‘show’ them, and barging into their conversations before they’ve finished a thought.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n-
